md:px-10 lg:px-12 xl:px-20"><p class="not-prose -mt-2 mb-6 text-sm"><a class="text-muted-foreground underline underline-offset-4 transition-colors hover:text-foreground" href="/transcripts/home-cli-demo/">View transcript: Kiro CLI demo: starting a cloud session</a></p></div></div><div class="grid w-full grid-cols-12 gap-2 px-6 md:gap-4 md:px-10 lg:px-12 xl:px-20 xl:gap-6 max-w-none xl-custom:max-w-[1480px] mx-auto"><section class="col-span-12 mb-30" data-component="secondary-feature"><div class="flex flex-col"><div class="mb-[4rem] flex w-full flex-col justify-center overflow-hidden last:mb-0 lg:flex-row-reverse"><div class="relative mb-[1.5rem] w-full overflow-hidden rounded-[32px] lg:w-[50%]"><div class="relative aspect-[3/2]"><div class="absolute inset-0 flex animate-pulse items-center justify-center bg-muted"><span class="sr-only">Loading image...</span></div><img alt="A task management interface showing a hierarchical checklist for setting up a TypeScript/Bun project. Task 1 covers project initialization—creating package.json (with playwright, fast-check), tsconfig.json, bunfig.toml, and a src/ directory structure. Task 2 covers implementing a CLI entry point (src/index.ts) that reads a target URL from Bun.argv, validates it, and reads an API_KEY environment variable. All tasks are unchecked with a Run all tasks button at top-right." loading="eager" decoding="async" data-nimg="fill" class="transition-opacity duration-300 opacity-0 object-contain" style="position:absolute;height:100%;width:100%;left:0;top:0;right:0;bottom:0;color:transparent" src="/images/home/secondary-specs.png?h=a4ddb0c4"/></div></div><div class="flex flex-col justify-center gap-6 lg:w-[50%]"><h3 class="md:max-w-[80%]">Bring structure to AI coding with spec-driven development</h3><div class="text-base text-prey-300 md:max-w-[80%]"><p>Kiro turns your prompts into <a class="underline transition-colors hover:text-prey-200 focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-purple-500 focus:ring-offset-2 focus:ring-offset-prey-900" href="/docs/specs/">requirements, architectural designs, and sequenced tasks</a>, then implements them with <a class="underline transition-colors hover:text-prey-200 focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-purple-500 focus:ring-offset-2 focus:ring-offset-prey-900" href="/docs/chat/subagents/">parallel agents</a>. <a class="underline transition-colors hover:text-prey-200 focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-purple-500 focus:ring-offset-2 focus:ring-offset-prey-900" href="/docs/specs/correctness/">Property-based tests</a> catch the edge cases that pass unit tests but break in production. With spec-driven development, you ship maintainable code that matches what you specified.</p></div></div></div><div class="mb-[4rem] flex w-full flex-col justify-center overflow-hidden last:mb-0 lg:flex-row-reverse"><div class="relative mb-[1.5rem] w-full overflow-hidden rounded-[32px] lg:w-[50%]"><div class="relative aspect-[3/2]"><div class="absolute inset-0 flex animate-pulse items-center justify-center bg-muted"><span class="sr-only">Loading image...</span></div><img alt="Kiro spec card titled Property 2: Ticket uniqueness. Description reads: For any sequence of parking operations, all generated ticket IDs should be unique. Validates Requirement 1.2. Implementation task: 3.2 Write property test for ticket uniqueness." loading="eager" decoding="async" data-nimg="fill" class="transition-opacity duration-300 opacity-0 object-contain" style="position:absolute;height:100%;width:100%;left:0;top:0;right:0;bottom:0;color:transparent" src="/images/home/secondary-pbt.png?h=8da4c996"/></div></div><div class="flex flex-col justify-center gap-6 lg:w-[50%]"><h3 class="md:max-w-[80%]">Check correctness to catch issues missed by unit tests</h3><div class="text-base text-prey-300 md:max-w-[80%]"><p>“All tests passed” doesn’t mean the code matches your intent. Before writing code, Kiro checks your <a class="underline transition-colors hover:text-prey-200 focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-purple-500 focus:ring-offset-2 focus:ring-offset-prey-900" href="/docs/specs/feature-specs/">requirements</a> for contradictions and gaps using automated reasoning techniques. Then it checks behavior with <a class="underline transition-colors hover:text-prey-200 focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-purple-500 focus:ring-offset-2 focus:ring-offset-prey-900" href="/docs/specs/correctness/">property-based tests</a> (like fuzz testing) which assert rules that must hold across all inputs instead of checking a few examples.</p></div></div></div><div class="mb-[4rem] flex w-full flex-col justify-center overflow-hidden last:mb-0 lg:flex-row-reverse"><div class="relative mb-[1.5rem] w-full overflow-hidden rounded-[32px] lg:w-[50%]"><div class="relative aspect-[3/2]"><div class="absolute inset-0 flex animate-pulse items-center justify-center bg-muted"><span class="sr-only">Loading image...</span></div><img alt="Logos of Figma, Postman, Datadog, Miro, Stripe, Supabase, ElevenLabs, Google Firebase - GitHub, GitLab, CI/CD" loading="eager" decoding="async" data-nimg="fill" class="transition-opacity duration-300 opacity-0 object-contain" style="position:absolute;height:100%;width:100%;left:0;top:0;right:0;bottom:0;color:transparent" src="/images/home/secondary-powers.png?h=997d927f"/></div></div><div class="flex flex-col justify-center gap-6 lg:w-[50%]"><h3 class="md:max-w-[80%]">Alongside you from plan to PR</h3><div class="text-base text-prey-300 md:max-w-[80%]"><p>Plan with <a class="underline transition-colors hover:text-prey-200 focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-purple-500 focus:ring-offset-2 focus:ring-offset-prey-900" href="/docs/specs/">specs</a>. Implement with <a class="underline transition-colors hover:text-prey-200 focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-purple-500 focus:ring-offset-2 focus:ring-offset-prey-900" href="/docs/chat/subagents/">parallel agents</a>, on your local machine or in the cloud. Catch bugs with <a class="underline transition-colors hover:text-prey-200 focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-purple-500 focus:ring-offset-2 focus:ring-offset-prey-900" href="/docs/specs/correctness/">property-based tests</a>, or pull context from tools like <a class="underline transition-colors hover:text-prey-200 focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-purple-500 focus:ring-offset-2 focus:ring-offset-prey-900" href="/powers/#figma">Figma</a> or <a class="underline transition-colors hover:text-prey-200 focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-purple-500 focus:ring-offset-2 focus:ring-offset-prey-900" href="/powers/#terraform">Terraform</a>. Run the <a class="underline transition-colors hover:text-prey-200 focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-purple-500 focus:ring-offset-2 focus:ring-offset-prey-900" href="/docs/cli/headless/">headless CLI in CI/CD</a> to review PRs and fix bugs without opening an editor.</p></div></div></div><div class="mb-[4rem] flex w-full flex-col justify-center overflow-hidden last:mb-0 lg:flex-row-reverse"><div class="relative mb-[1.5rem] w-full overflow-hidden rounded-[32px] lg:w-[50%]"><div class="relative aspect-[3/2]"><div class="absolute inset-0 flex animate-pulse items-center justify-center bg-muted"><span class="sr-only">Loading image...</span></div><img alt="Kiro model selector dropdown showing available AI models with credit multipliers: Auto at 1x, Claude Opus 4.8, 4.7, 4.6, and 4.5 at 2.2x, Claude Sonnet 4.6, 4.5, and 4 at 1.3x, Claude Haiku 4.5 at 0.4x, Deepseek v3.2 at 0.25x, and MiniMax M2.5 at 0.25x. Claude Opus 4.8 is selected with an xhigh quality designation and an Edit button. The Autopilot toggle is enabled at the bottom of the input field." loading="eager" decoding="async" data-nimg="fill" class="transition-opacity duration-300 opacity-0 object-contain" style="position:absolute;height:100%;width:100%;left:0;top:0;right:0;bottom:0;color:transparent" src="/images/home/secondary-models.png?h=47c83457"/></div></div><div class="flex flex-col justify-center gap-6 lg:w-[50%]"><h3 class="md:max-w-[80%]">Powered by your favorite models</h3><div class="text-base text-prey-300 md:max-w-[80%]"><p>Choose the best model for every task: Anthropic Claude, open-weight models, or <a class="underline transition-colors hover:text-prey-200 focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-purple-500 focus:ring-offset-2 focus:ring-offset-prey-900" href="/docs/models/#auto-recommended">Auto</a>, which picks the best model for the task based on complexity, factoring in model quality, latency, and cost.</p></div><a class="flex items-center gap-1 The Kiro IDE supports <a class="underline hover:text-prey-200" href="/docs/editor/extension-registry/">Open VSX extensions</a>, themes, and <a class="underline hover:text-prey-200" href="/docs/guides/migrating-from-vscode/">VS Code settings</a>. <a class="underline hover:text-prey-200" href="/cli/">Kiro CLI</a> works with bash, zsh, fish, and <a class="underline hover:text-prey-200" href="/docs/cli/autocomplete/">over 500 popular CLIs</a>.</p></div></div><div class="flex flex-col"><div class="mb-4 flex flex-row items-center gap-3"><div class="flex href="/docs/privacy-and-security/">security, reliability, and privacy</a> standards and controls trusted by millions of customers. Kiro offers <a class="underline hover:text-prey-200" href="/docs/enterprise/identity-provider/">IAM and SSO authentication</a>, usage dashboards, cost management controls, IP indemnity, <a class="underline hover:text-prey-200" href="/docs/enterprise/governance/">governance</a>, and administration controls you’d expect from an enterprise-grade product.</p></div><div class="mt-4"><a class="mb-0">Kiro offers a <a class="underline hover:text-prey-200" href="/pricing/#what-is-a-credit">credit-based</a> pricing model with no daily or weekly rate limits and pre-paid overages, so you class="text-prey-300">Principal Software Engineer</p></div></a></div></div><div class="flex-[0_0_350px]"><p class="mb-4 w-full rounded-lg border border-prey-700 px-8 py-6">Kiro isn’t just speeding up my work – it’s making me write better code, think more systematically about architecture, and deliver higher quality results while automated workflows happen seamlessly in the background.</p><div class="flex max-w-[350px] gap-2 px-8"><a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="flex cursor-pointer gap-2 transition-opacity hover:opacity-80" bg-muted">AN</span></span><div class="text-utility-lg"><p>Anuj Tyagi</p><p class="text-prey-300">Developer</p></div></a></div></div><div class="flex-[0_0_350px]"><p class="mb-4 w-full rounded-lg border border-prey-700 px-8 py-6">Kiro is just more than code. Aside from just listening to prompts and doing things, it can actually work on tasks based on predefined triggers, making the development experience more efficient.</p><div class="flex max-w-[350px] gap-2 px-8"><a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="flex cursor-pointer gap-2 transition-opacity hover:opacity-80" href="https://blog.sreekeshiyer.com/"><span class="relative flex shrink-0 overflow-hidden rounded-full h-10 w-10"><span class="flex h-full w-full items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-muted">SR</span></span><div class="text-utility-lg"><p>Sreekesh Iyer</p><p class="text-prey-300">Software Engineer</p></div></a></div></div><div class="flex-[0_0_350px]"><p class="mb-4 w-full rounded-lg border border-prey-700 px-8 py-6">As a startup co-founder and CTO, time is the most important resource. Kiro justifies the use of my time for developing our business critical assets in-house.</p><div class="flex max-w-[350px] gap-2 px-8"><span class="relative flex shrink-0 overflow-hidden rounded-full h-10 w-10"><span class="flex h-full w-full items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-muted">RO</span></span><div class="text-utility-lg"><p>Rolf Koski</p><p class="text-prey-300">CTO &amp; Co-Founder</p></div></div></div><div class="flex-[0_0_350px]"><p class="mb-4 w-full rounded-lg border border-prey-700 px-8 py-6">In my role designing AWS Cloud and AI solutions with Terraform and Python, spec-driven development with Kiro has brought code relevancy and quality to a whole new level. We’ve accelerated feature development dramatically, reducing time to customer value from weeks to days. We are excited to welcome Kiro as our newest team member.</p><div class="flex max-w-[350px] gap-2 px-8"><span class="relative flex shrink-0 overflow-hidden rounded-full h-10 w-10"><span class="flex h-full w-full items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-muted">HÅ</span></span><div class="text-utility-lg"><p>Håkon Eriksen Drange</p><p class="text-prey-300">Principal Cloud Architect</p></div></div></div><div class="flex-[0_0_350px]"><p class="mb-4 w-full rounded-lg border border-prey-700 px-8 py-6">Kiro is a strong ally for startups. It naturally turns overlooked docs and specs into robust assets, making growth smoother and future scaling more effective.</p><div class="flex max-w-[350px] gap-2 px-8"><span class="relative flex shrink-0 overflow-hidden rounded-full h-10 w-10"><span class="flex h-full w-full items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-muted">KE</span></span><div class="text-utility-lg"><p>Kento Ikeda</p><p class="text-prey-300">Founder &amp; Engineer</p></div></div></div><div class="flex-[0_0_350px]"><p class="mb-4 w-full rounded-lg border border-prey-700 px-8 py-6">I use Kiro for everything - drafting new Terraform modules, tweaking container setups, even writing down random AI ideas at 2 am. But more than anything, it supports how I learn. I’m endlessly curious, and Kiro helps me stay in that learning loop—tinkering, breaking things, fixing them, and then allowing me to share what I learn back with the community.</p><div class="flex max-w-[350px] gap-2 px-8"><span class="relative flex shrink-0 overflow-hidden rounded-full h-10 w-10"><span class="flex h-full w-full items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-muted">AD</span></span><div class="text-utility-lg"><p>Adit Modi</p><p class="text-prey-300">Solution Architect</p></div></div></div><div class="flex-[0_0_350px]"><p class="mb-4 w-full rounded-lg border border-prey-700 px-8 py-6">I’ve been blown away by Kiro’s capabilities. The agentic experience is really transformative. From the multimodal inputs that understand context to the complete lifecycle control within the IDE, it feels like I’m working with a senior developer.</p><div class="flex max-w-[350px] gap-2 px-8"><a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="flex cursor-pointer gap-2 transition-opacity hover:opacity-80" href="http://dev.to/vivek-aws"><span class="relative flex shrink-0 overflow-hidden rounded-full h-10 w-10"><span class="flex h-full w-full items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-muted">VI</span></span><div class="text-utility-lg"><p>Vivek Velso</p><p class="text-prey-300">Cloud Engineering Lead</p></div></a></div></div><div class="flex-[0_0_350px]"><p class="mb-4 w-full rounded-lg border border-prey-700 px-8 py-6">Most tools are great at generating code, but Kiro gives structure to the chaos before you write a single line.</p><div class="flex max-w-[350px] gap-2 px-8"><a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="flex cursor-pointer gap-2 transition-opacity hover:opacity-80" href="https://medium.com/abdifarah076"><span class="relative flex shrink-0 overflow-hidden rounded-full h-10 w-10"><span class="flex h-full w-full items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-muted">FA</span></span><div class="text-utility-lg"><p>Farah Abdirahman</p><p class="text-prey-300">Cloud &amp; AI Engineer</p></div></a></div></div><div class="flex-[0_0_350px]"><p class="mb-4 w-full rounded-lg border border-prey-700 px-8 py-6">In roughly two days, I built a secure file sharing application from scratch. By simply sharing my requirements with Kiro, I was able to create a fully secure application that incorporates encryption and various security coding practices—no additional prompts needed.</p><div class="flex max-w-[350px] gap-2 px-8"><a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="flex cursor-pointer gap-2 transition-opacity hover:opacity-80" href="https://github.com/igorsasovets"><span class="relative flex shrink-0 overflow-hidden rounded-full h-10 w-10"><span class="flex h-full w-full items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-muted">IH</span></span><div class="text-utility-lg"><p>Ihor Sasovets</p><p class="text-prey-300">Lead Security Engineer</p></div></a></div></div><div class="flex-[0_0_350px]"><p class="mb-4 w-full rounded-lg border border-prey-700 px-8 py-6">I often forget to add unit tests, or update documentation when pushing changes, but with Kiro I can create a hook and it will automatically run those tasks in the background for me, never having to think twice.</p><div class="flex max-w-[350px] gap-2 px-8"><a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="flex cursor-pointer gap-2 transition-opacity hover:opacity-80" href="https://github.com/dashapetr"><span class="relative flex shrink-0 overflow-hidden rounded-full h-10 w-10"><span class="flex h-full w-full items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-muted">DA</span></span><div class="text-utility-lg"><p>Darya Petrashka</p><p class="text-prey-300">Senior Data Scientist</p></div></a></div></div><div class="flex-[0_0_350px]"><p class="mb-4 w-full rounded-lg border border-prey-700 px-8 py-6">As an open source developer, I typically don’t build games, but last night I was able to create one using Kiro. I didn’t have to think too hard about implementation since Kiro handled the logic and enabled me to write code quickly and accurately.</p><div class="flex max-w-[350px] gap-2 px-8"><a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="flex cursor-pointer gap-2 transition-opacity hover:opacity-80" href="https://github.com/walmsles"><span class="relative flex shrink-0 overflow-hidden rounded-full h-10 w-10"><span class="flex h-full w-full items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-muted">MI</span></span><div class="text-utility-lg"><p>Michael Walmsley</p><p class="text-prey-300">Serverless Application Architect</p></div></a></div></div><div class="flex-[0_0_350px]"><p class="mb-4 w-full rounded-lg border border-prey-700 px-8 py-6">Kiro lowers the barrier to entry, whether that be, you don’t even know how to code, you’re new to coding, or you’re like me and you’re just learning a new tech stack. If something doesn’t make sense to you, or you feel like you are asking a silly question, ask Kiro anyway!</p><div class="flex max-w-[350px] gap-2 px-8"><a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="flex cursor-pointer gap-2 transition-opacity hover:opacity-80" href="https://github.com/deeheber"><span class="relative flex shrink-0 overflow-hidden rounded-full h-10 w-10"><span class="flex h-full w-full items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-muted">DA</span></span><div class="text-utility-lg"><p>Danielle Heberling</p><p class="text-prey-300">Founding Infrastructure my specs, and builds me the application I want, faster.</p><div class="flex max-w-[350px] gap-2 px-8"><a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="flex cursor-pointer gap-2 transition-opacity How is it different from vibe coding?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"\u003Cp\u003EDeveloping with specs keeps the fun of vibe coding, but fixes some of its limitations: vibe coding can require too much guidance on complex tasks or when building on top of large codebases, and it can misinterpret context. When implementing a task with vibe coding, it’s difficult to keep track of all the decisions that were made along the way, and document them for your team. By using specs, Kiro works alongside you to define requirements, system design, and tasks to be implemented before writing any code. This approach explicitly documents the reasoning and implementation decisions, so Kiro can implement more complex tasks in fewer shots.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\n"}},{"@type":"Question","name":"How can I get started with Kiro?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"\u003Cp\u003EKiro works as an agent in the \u003Ca href=\"\u002Fdocs\u002F\"\u003EIDE\u003C\u002Fa\u003E, with a \u003Ca href=\"\u002Fdocs\u002Fcli\u002F\"\u003ECLI\u003C\u002Fa\u003E, in a \u003Ca href=\"\u002Fdocs\u002Fweb\u002F\"\u003Eweb browser\u003C\u002Fa\u003E, from mobile app, and in Kiro Crew. \u003Ca href=\"\u002Fdownloads\u002F\"\u003EDownload\u003C\u002Fa\u003E the installer for your operating system, or \u003Ca href=\"https:\u002F\u002Fapp.kiro.dev\u002F\"\u003Esign in\u003C\u002Fa\u003E to use it with a paid account on the web or mobile. Log in with GitHub, Google, AWS Builder ID, or AWS IAM Identity Center. You do not need an AWS account to use Kiro. Kiro Crew is an open-source development workspace that runs on your machine and keeps working across sessions, it’s free, install from \u003Ca href=\"https:\u002F\u002Fgithub.com\u002Fkirodotdev\u002FKiroCrew\"\u003EGitHub\u003C\u002Fa\u003E. For more, see \u003Ca href=\"\u002Fdocs\"\u003Edocumentation\u003C\u002Fa\u003E.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\n"}},{"@type":"Question","name":"What programming languages does Kiro support?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"\u003Cp\u003EKiro supports a variety of programming languages that developers use in their day to day work. This list includes, but is not limited to Python, Java, JavaScript, TypeScript, C#, Go, Rust, PHP, Ruby, Kotlin, C, C++, shell scripting, SQL, Scala, JSON, YAML, and HCL.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\n"}},{"@type":"Question","name":"Can I import settings from my existing IDE?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"\u003Cp\u003EKiro is based on Code OSS, so you can \u003Ca href=\"\u002Fdocs\u002Fguides\u002Fmigrating-from-vscode\u002F\"\u003Eimport your VS Code settings, themes, and Open VSX compatible plugins\u003C\u002Fa\u003E in the Kiro onboarding flow.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\n"}},{"@type":"Question","name":"How is Kiro Web different from Kiro IDE, CLI, and Kiro Crew?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"\u003Cp\u003EKiro IDE is for active, local development with real-time agent collaboration. Kiro CLI is for terminal-based workflows, custom agents, and deployment pipelines. Kiro Crew is the persistent, open source development workspace that coordinates across your unique tools and workflows. \u003Ca href=\"\u002Fweb\u002F\"\u003EKiro Web\u003C\u002Fa\u003E is for delegating and steering from the browser, with sessions that run in isolated cloud \u003Ca href=\"\u002Fdocs\u002Fweb\u002Fsandbox\u002F\"\u003Esandboxes\u003C\u002Fa\u003E, keep going when you close your laptop, and coordinate changes across \u003Ca href=\"\u002Fdocs\u002Fweb\u002Fgithub\u002F\"\u003EGitHub\u003C\u002Fa\u003E and \u003Ca href=\"\u002Fdocs\u002Fweb\u002Fgitlab\u002F\"\u003EGitLab\u003C\u002Fa\u003E repos in a single session. All three modes (\u003Ca href=\"\u002Fdocs\u002Fweb\u002Fautonomous-mode\u002F\"\u003Eautonomous\u003C\u002Fa\u003E, \u003Ca href=\"\u002Fdocs\u002Fspecs\u002F\"\u003Espec\u003C\u002Fa\u003E, and default) are available together in Kiro on the web. \u003Ca href=\"\u002Fdocs\u002Fweb\u002Fautomations\u002F\"\u003ECloud automations\u003C\u002Fa\u003E are only available in Kiro on the web today. Use whichever Kiro interface fits the work; they work best together. \u003Ca href=\"\u002Fdocs\u002Fweb\u002Fsteering\u002F\"\u003ESteering files\u003C\u002Fa\u003E carry across all surfaces, and personal configuration syncs through \u003Ca href=\"\u002Fdocs\u002Fweb\u002Fcloud-configuration\u002F\"\u003Ecloud configuration\u003C\u002Fa\u003E, so your setup follows you regardless of where you work.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\n"}},{"@type":"Question","name":"What is an AI agent?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"\u003Cp\u003EAn agent is an AI system that can plan and execute multi-step tasks with some degree of autonomy. Unlike simple chatbots that respond to individual prompts, agents can break down complex tasks, make decisions about how to proceed, and take actions to accomplish goals.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\n"}}],"dateModified":"2026-09-12T20:52:45.109Z"}</script><section id="faqs" class="col-span-12 scroll-mt-24 md:col-span-8 md:col-start-3 mb-20 lg:mb-30" data-component="faq"><h2 class="pb-6 md:text-center">Common questions</h2><div class="flex w-full flex-col"><div data-orientation="vertical"><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="what-is-kiro" class="border-b border-prey-700" data-faq-question="What is Kiro?" data-faq-answer="Kiro is an agentic AI with an [IDE](/docs/), [CLI](/docs/cli/), [web interface](/docs/web/), mobile app, and Kiro Crew that helps developers and teams do their best work. From quick fixes to complex tasks, Kiro works alongside you to turn prompts into executable specs, validate code correctness to find bugs unit tests miss, and build across large codebases with parallel agents that learn from every session—so what you build is exactly what you want and ready to share with your team. Kiro agents help you solve challenging problems, automate tasks, and keep working in the cloud when you step away.
"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_9kvav5aivb_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="radix-_R_1kvav5aivb_" class="group flex w-full items-center justify-between gap-4 py-4 text-start text-h4" data-radix-collection-item=""><span class="flex-1 text-left transition-colors hover:text-prey-200">What is Kiro?</span><div class="relative flex items-center justify-center overflow-hidden rounded-lg bg-transparent px-2 py-4 transition-all"><span class="relative z-20 flex items-center justify-center"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none"><path d="M12 5V12V19M5 12H19" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"></path></svg></span><span class="absolute inset-0 z-10 rounded-lg bg-purple-500 transition-transform duration-500 md:translate-y-[50%] md:scale-0 translate-y-[50%] scale-0 md:translate-y-[50%] md:scale-0 md:group-hover:translate-y-0 md:group-hover:scale-x-[150%] md:group-hover:scale-y-[220%]"></span></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_9kvav5aivb_" hidden="" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_1kvav5aivb_" data-orientation="vertical" class="faq-content" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"></div><div class="faq-crawler-content sr-only" aria-hidden="true"><h3>What is Kiro?</h3><div>Kiro is an agentic AI with an [IDE](/docs/), [CLI](/docs/cli/), [web interface](/docs/web/), mobile app, and Kiro Crew that helps developers and teams do their best work. From quick fixes to complex tasks, Kiro works alongside you to turn prompts into executable specs, validate code correctness to find bugs unit tests miss, and build across large codebases with parallel agents that learn from every session—so what you build is exactly what you want and ready to share with your team. Kiro agents help you solve challenging problems, automate tasks, and keep working in the cloud when you step away.
</div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="what-is-specdriven-development-how-is-it-different-from-vibe-coding" class="border-b border-prey-700" data-faq-question="What is spec-driven development? How is it different from vibe coding?" data-faq-answer="Developing with specs keeps the fun of vibe coding, but fixes some of its limitations: vibe coding can require too much guidance on complex tasks or when building on top of large codebases, and it can misinterpret context. When implementing a task with vibe coding, it’s difficult to keep track of all the decisions that were made along the way, and document them for your team. By using specs, Kiro works alongside you to define requirements, system design, and tasks to be implemented before writing any code. This approach explicitly documents the reasoning and implementation decisions, so Kiro can implement more complex tasks in fewer shots.
"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_akvav5aivb_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="radix-_R_2kvav5aivb_" class="group flex w-full items-center justify-between gap-4 py-4 text-start text-h4" data-radix-collection-item=""><span class="flex-1 text-left transition-colors hover:text-prey-200">What is spec-driven development? How is it different from vibe coding?</span><div class="relative flex items-center justify-center overflow-hidden rounded-lg bg-transparent px-2 py-4 transition-all"><span class="relative z-20 flex items-center justify-center"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none"><path d="M12 5V12V19M5 12H19" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"></path></svg></span><span class="absolute inset-0 z-10 rounded-lg bg-purple-500 transition-transform duration-500 md:translate-y-[50%] md:scale-0 translate-y-[50%] scale-0 md:translate-y-[50%] md:scale-0 md:group-hover:translate-y-0 md:group-hover:scale-x-[150%] md:group-hover:scale-y-[220%]"></span></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_akvav5aivb_" hidden="" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_2kvav5aivb_" data-orientation="vertical" class="faq-content" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"></div><div class="faq-crawler-content sr-only" aria-hidden="true"><h3>What is spec-driven development? How is it different from vibe coding?</h3><div>Developing with specs keeps the fun of vibe coding, but fixes some of its limitations: vibe coding can require too much guidance on complex tasks or when building on top of large codebases, and it can misinterpret context. When implementing a task with vibe coding, it’s difficult to keep track of all the decisions that were made along the way, and document them for your team. By using specs, Kiro works alongside you to define requirements, system design, and tasks to be implemented before writing any code. This approach explicitly documents the reasoning and implementation decisions, so Kiro can implement more complex tasks in fewer shots.
</div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="how-can-i-get-started-with-kiro" class="border-b border-prey-700" data-faq-question="How can I get started with Kiro?" data-faq-answer="Kiro works as an agent in the [IDE](/docs/), with a [CLI](/docs/cli/), in a [web browser](/docs/web/), from mobile app, and in Kiro Crew. [Download](/downloads/) the installer for your operating system, or [sign in](https://app.kiro.dev/) to use it with a paid account on the web or mobile. Log in with GitHub, Google, AWS Builder ID, or AWS IAM Identity Center. You do not need an AWS account to use Kiro. Kiro Crew is an open-source development workspace that runs on your machine and keeps working across sessions, it’s free, install from [GitHub](https://github.com/kirodotdev/KiroCrew). For more, see [documentation](/docs).
"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_bkvav5aivb_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="radix-_R_3kvav5aivb_" class="group flex w-full items-center justify-between gap-4 py-4 text-start text-h4" data-radix-collection-item=""><span class="flex-1 text-left transition-colors hover:text-prey-200">How can I get started with Kiro?</span><div class="relative flex items-center justify-center overflow-hidden rounded-lg bg-transparent px-2 py-4 transition-all"><span class="relative z-20 flex items-center justify-center"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none"><path d="M12 5V12V19M5 12H19" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"></path></svg></span><span class="absolute inset-0 z-10 rounded-lg bg-purple-500 transition-transform duration-500 md:translate-y-[50%] md:scale-0 translate-y-[50%] scale-0 md:translate-y-[50%] md:scale-0 md:group-hover:translate-y-0 md:group-hover:scale-x-[150%] md:group-hover:scale-y-[220%]"></span></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_bkvav5aivb_" hidden="" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_3kvav5aivb_" data-orientation="vertical" class="faq-content" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"></div><div class="faq-crawler-content sr-only" aria-hidden="true"><h3>How can I get started with Kiro?</h3><div>Kiro works as an agent in the [IDE](/docs/), with a [CLI](/docs/cli/), in a [web browser](/docs/web/), from mobile app, and in Kiro Crew. [Download](/downloads/) the installer for your operating system, or [sign in](https://app.kiro.dev/) to use it with a paid account on the web or mobile. Log in with GitHub, Google, AWS Builder ID, or AWS IAM Identity Center. You do not need an AWS account to use Kiro. Kiro Crew is an open-source development workspace that runs on your machine and keeps working across sessions, it’s free, install from [GitHub](https://github.com/kirodotdev/KiroCrew). For more, see [documentation](/docs).
</div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="what-programming-languages-does-kiro-support" class="border-b border-prey-700" data-faq-question="What programming languages does Kiro support?" data-faq-answer="Kiro supports a variety of programming languages that developers use in their day to day work. This list includes, but is not limited to Python, Java, JavaScript, TypeScript, C#, Go, Rust, PHP, Ruby, Kotlin, C, C++, shell scripting, SQL, Scala, JSON, YAML, and HCL.
"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_ckvav5aivb_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="radix-_R_4kvav5aivb_" class="group flex w-full items-center justify-between gap-4 py-4 text-start text-h4" data-radix-collection-item=""><span class="flex-1 text-left transition-colors hover:text-prey-200">What programming languages does Kiro support?</span><div class="relative flex items-center justify-center overflow-hidden rounded-lg bg-transparent px-2 py-4 transition-all"><span class="relative z-20 flex items-center justify-center"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none"><path d="M12 5V12V19M5 12H19" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"></path></svg></span><span class="absolute inset-0 z-10 rounded-lg bg-purple-500 transition-transform duration-500 md:translate-y-[50%] md:scale-0 translate-y-[50%] scale-0 md:translate-y-[50%] md:scale-0 md:group-hover:translate-y-0 md:group-hover:scale-x-[150%] md:group-hover:scale-y-[220%]"></span></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_ckvav5aivb_" hidden="" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_4kvav5aivb_" data-orientation="vertical" class="faq-content" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"></div><div class="faq-crawler-content sr-only" aria-hidden="true"><h3>What programming languages does Kiro support?</h3><div>Kiro supports a variety of programming languages that developers use in their day to day work. This list includes, but is not limited to Python, Java, JavaScript, TypeScript, C#, Go, Rust, PHP, Ruby, Kotlin, C, C++, shell scripting, SQL, Scala, JSON, YAML, and HCL.
</div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="can-i-import-settings-from-my-existing-ide" class="border-b border-prey-700" data-faq-question="Can I import settings from my existing IDE?" data-faq-answer="Kiro is based on Code OSS, so you can [import your VS Code settings, themes, and Open VSX compatible plugins](/docs/guides/migrating-from-vscode/) in the Kiro onboarding flow.
"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_dkvav5aivb_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="radix-_R_5kvav5aivb_" class="group flex w-full items-center justify-between gap-4 py-4 text-start text-h4" data-radix-collection-item=""><span class="flex-1 text-left transition-colors hover:text-prey-200">Can I import settings from my existing IDE?</span><div class="relative flex items-center justify-center overflow-hidden rounded-lg bg-transparent px-2 py-4 transition-all"><span class="relative z-20 flex items-center justify-center"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none"><path d="M12 5V12V19M5 12H19" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"></path></svg></span><span class="absolute inset-0 z-10 rounded-lg bg-purple-500 transition-transform duration-500 md:translate-y-[50%] md:scale-0 translate-y-[50%] scale-0 md:translate-y-[50%] md:scale-0 md:group-hover:translate-y-0 md:group-hover:scale-x-[150%] md:group-hover:scale-y-[220%]"></span></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_dkvav5aivb_" hidden="" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_5kvav5aivb_" data-orientation="vertical" class="faq-content" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"></div><div class="faq-crawler-content sr-only" aria-hidden="true"><h3>Can I import settings from my existing IDE?</h3><div>Kiro is based on Code OSS, so you can [import your VS Code settings, themes, and Open VSX compatible plugins](/docs/guides/migrating-from-vscode/) in the Kiro onboarding flow.
</div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="how-is-kiro-web-different-from-kiro-ide-cli-and-kiro-crew" class="border-b border-prey-700" data-faq-question="How is Kiro Web different from Kiro IDE, CLI, and Kiro Crew?" data-faq-answer="Kiro IDE is for active, local development with real-time agent collaboration. Kiro CLI is for terminal-based workflows, custom agents, and deployment pipelines. Kiro Crew is the persistent, open source development workspace that coordinates across your unique tools and workflows. [Kiro Web](/web/) is for delegating and steering from the browser, with sessions that run in isolated cloud [sandboxes](/docs/web/sandbox/), keep going when you close your laptop, and coordinate changes across [GitHub](/docs/web/github/) and [GitLab](/docs/web/gitlab/) repos in a single session. All three modes ([autonomous](/docs/web/autonomous-mode/), [spec](/docs/specs/), and default) are available together in Kiro on the web. [Cloud automations](/docs/web/automations/) are only available in Kiro on the web today. Use whichever Kiro interface fits the work; they work best together. [Steering files](/docs/web/steering/) carry across all surfaces, and personal configuration syncs through [cloud configuration](/docs/web/cloud-configuration/), so your setup follows you regardless of where you work.
"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_ekvav5aivb_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="radix-_R_6kvav5aivb_" class="group flex w-full items-center justify-between gap-4 py-4 text-start text-h4" data-radix-collection-item=""><span class="flex-1 text-left transition-colors hover:text-prey-200">How is Kiro Web different from Kiro IDE, CLI, and Kiro Crew?</span><div class="relative flex items-center justify-center overflow-hidden rounded-lg bg-transparent px-2 py-4 transition-all"><span class="relative z-20 flex items-center justify-center"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none"><path d="M12 5V12V19M5 12H19" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"></path></svg></span><span class="absolute inset-0 z-10 rounded-lg bg-purple-500 transition-transform duration-500 md:translate-y-[50%] md:scale-0 translate-y-[50%] scale-0 md:translate-y-[50%] md:scale-0 md:group-hover:translate-y-0 md:group-hover:scale-x-[150%] md:group-hover:scale-y-[220%]"></span></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_ekvav5aivb_" hidden="" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_6kvav5aivb_" data-orientation="vertical" class="faq-content" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"></div><div class="faq-crawler-content sr-only" aria-hidden="true"><h3>How is Kiro Web different from Kiro IDE, CLI, and Kiro Crew?</h3><div>Kiro IDE is for active, local development with real-time agent collaboration. Kiro CLI is for terminal-based workflows, custom agents, and deployment pipelines. Kiro Crew is the persistent, open source development workspace that coordinates across your unique tools and workflows. [Kiro Web](/web/) is for delegating and steering from the browser, with sessions that run in isolated cloud [sandboxes](/docs/web/sandbox/), keep going when you close your laptop, and coordinate changes across [GitHub](/docs/web/github/) and [GitLab](/docs/web/gitlab/) repos in a single session. All three modes ([autonomous](/docs/web/autonomous-mode/), [spec](/docs/specs/), and default) are available together in Kiro on the web. [Cloud automations](/docs/web/automations/) are only available in Kiro on the web today. Use whichever Kiro interface fits the work; they work best together. [Steering files](/docs/web/steering/) carry across all surfaces, and personal configuration syncs through [cloud configuration](/docs/web/cloud-configuration/), so your setup follows you regardless of where you work.
</div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="what-is-an-ai-agent" class="border-b border-prey-700" data-faq-question="What is an AI agent?" data-faq-answer="An agent is an AI system that can plan and execute multi-step tasks with some degree of autonomy. Unlike simple chatbots that respond to individual prompts, agents can break down complex tasks, make decisions about how to proceed, and take actions to accomplish goals.
"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_fkvav5aivb_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="radix-_R_7kvav5aivb_" class="group flex w-full items-center justify-between gap-4 py-4 text-start text-h4" data-radix-collection-item=""><span class="flex-1 text-left transition-colors hover:text-prey-200">What is an AI agent?</span><div class="relative flex items-center justify-center overflow-hidden rounded-lg bg-transparent px-2 py-4 transition-all"><span class="relative z-20 flex items-center justify-center"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none"><path d="M12 5V12V19M5 12H19" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"></path></svg></span><span class="absolute inset-0 z-10 rounded-lg bg-purple-500 transition-transform duration-500 md:translate-y-[50%] md:scale-0 translate-y-[50%] scale-0 md:translate-y-[50%] md:scale-0 md:group-hover:translate-y-0 md:group-hover:scale-x-[150%] md:group-hover:scale-y-[220%]"></span></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_fkvav5aivb_" hidden="" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_7kvav5aivb_" data-orientation="vertical" class="faq-content" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"></div><div class="faq-crawler-content sr-only" aria-hidden="true"><h3>What is an AI agent?</h3><div>An agent is an AI system that can plan and execute multi-step tasks with some degree of autonomy. Unlike simple chatbots that respond to individual prompts, agents can break down complex tasks, make decisions about how to proceed, and take actions to accomplish goals.

structure to AI coding with spec-driven development\",\"text\":\"Kiro turns your prompts into [requirements, architectural designs, and sequenced tasks](/docs/specs/), then implements them with [parallel agents](/docs/chat/subagents/). [Property-based tests](/docs/specs/correctness/) catch the edge cases that pass unit tests but break in production. With spec-driven development, you ship maintainable code that matches what you specified.\\n\",\"image\":\"/images/home/secondary-specs.png\",\"imageAltText\":\"A task management interface showing a hierarchical checklist for setting up a TypeScript/Bun project. Task 1 covers project initialization—creating package.json (with playwright, fast-check), tsconfig.json, bunfig.toml, and a src/ directory structure. Task 2 covers implementing a CLI entry point (src/index.ts) that reads a target URL from Bun.argv, validates it, and reads an API_KEY environment variable. All tasks are unchecked with a Run all tasks button at top-right.\"},{\"title\":\"Check correctness to catch issues missed by unit tests\",\"text\":\"“All tests passed” doesn’t mean the code matches your intent. Before writing code, Kiro checks your [requirements](/docs/specs/feature-specs/) for contradictions and gaps using automated reasoning techniques. Then it checks behavior with [property-based tests](/docs/specs/correctness/) (like fuzz testing) which assert rules that must hold across all inputs instead of checking a few examples.\\n\",\"image\":\"/images/home/secondary-pbt.png\",\"imageAltText\":\"Kiro spec card titled Property 2: Ticket uniqueness. Description reads: For any sequence of parking operations, all generated ticket IDs should be unique. Validates Requirement 1.2. Implementation task: 3.2 Write property test for ticket uniqueness.\"},{\"title\":\"Alongside you from plan to PR\",\"text\":\"Plan with [specs](/docs/specs/). Implement with [parallel agents](/docs/chat/subagents/), on your local machine or in the cloud. Catch bugs with [property-based tests](/docs/specs/correctness/), or pull context from tools like [Figma](/powers/#figma) or [Terraform](/powers/#terraform). Run the [headless CLI in CI/CD](/docs/cli/headless/) to review PRs and fix bugs without opening an editor.\\n\",\"image\":\"/images/home/secondary-powers.png\",\"imageAltText\":\"Logos of Figma, Postman, Datadog, Miro, Stripe, Supabase, ElevenLabs, Google Firebase - GitHub, GitLab, CI/CD\"},{\"title\":\"Powered by your favorite models\",\"text\":\"Choose the best model for every task: Anthropic Claude, open-weight models, or [Auto](/docs/models/#auto-recommended), which picks the best model for the task based on complexity, factoring in model quality, latency, and cost.\\n\",\"image\":\"/images/home/secondary-models.png\",\"imageAltText\":\"Kiro model selector dropdown showing available AI models with credit multipliers: Auto at 1x, Claude Opus 4.8, 4.7, 4.6, and 4.5 at 2.2x, Claude Sonnet 4.6, 4.5, and 4 at 1.3x, Claude Haiku 4.5 at 0.4x, Deepseek v3.2 at 0.25x, and MiniMax M2.5 at 0.25x. Claude Opus 4.8 is selected with an xhigh quality designation and an Edit button. The Autopilot toggle is enabled at the bottom of the input field.\",\"ctaText\":\"Learn about models\",\"ctaHref\":\"/docs/models\",\"ctaType\":\"link\"}]}]\n"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"36:[\"$\",\"div\",\"page-component-3\",{\"className\":\"w-full overflow-hidden\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L3c\",\"3\",{\"columns\":3,\"hasContainer\":false,\"iconColor\":\"purple\",\"itemsHeadlineAs\":\"h3\",\"customSpacing\":\"mt-16 md:mt-20 mb-44 md:mb-48\",\"centeredHeadline\":{\"headline\":\"Built for the way you work\"},\"items\":[{\"icon\":\"code-brackets\",\"title\":\"Built on open standards\",\"body\":\"The Kiro agent is compatible with [Agent Client Protocol (ACP)](/docs/cli/acp/) so you can bring it to your favorite tools. It supports AGENTS.md, [Skills.md](/docs/skills/), and [MCP](/docs/mcp/). The Kiro IDE supports [Open VSX extensions](/docs/editor/extension-registry/), themes, and [VS Code settings](/docs/guides/migrating-from-vscode/). [Kiro CLI](/cli/) works with bash, zsh, fish, and [over 500 popular CLIs](/docs/cli/autocomplete/).\"},{\"icon\":\"organization\",\"title\":\"Enterprise-ready\",\"body\":\"Built and operated by AWS, Kiro benefits from the same [security, reliability, and privacy](/docs/privacy-and-security/) standards and controls trusted by millions of customers. Kiro offers [IAM and SSO authentication](/docs/enterprise/identity-provider/), usage dashboards, cost management controls, IP indemnity, [governance](/docs/enterprise/governance/), and administration controls you’d expect from an enterprise-grade product.\",\"cta\":{\"text\":\"Kiro for enterprise\",\"href\":\"/enterprise\",\"type\":\"link\"}},{\"icon\":\"credit-card\",\"title\":\"Predictable pricing\",\"body\":\"Kiro offers a [credit-based](/pricing/#what-is-a-credit) pricing model with no daily or weekly rate limits and pre-paid overages, so you can code without breaking your flow or getting a surprise bill.\",\"cta\":{\"text\":\"View pricing\",\"href\":\"/pricing\",\"type\":\"link\"}}]}]}]\n"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"37:[\"$\",\"div\",\"page-component-4\",{\"className\":\"w-full overflow-hidden\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L3d\",\"4\",{\"headline\":\"Trusted by engineers worldwide\",\"items\":[{\"text\":\"As a startup co-founder and CTO, time is the most important resource. Kiro justifies the use of my time for developing our business critical assets in-house.\",\"author\":\"Rolf Koski\",\"title\":\"CTO \u0026 Co-Founder\",\"avatar\":\"/images/quotes/rolf-koski.png\",\"externalUrl\":\"\"},{\"text\":\"In my role designing AWS Cloud and AI solutions with Terraform and Python, spec-driven development with Kiro has brought code relevancy and quality to a whole new level. We’ve accelerated feature development dramatically, reducing time to customer value from weeks to days. We are excited to welcome Kiro as our newest team member.\",\"author\":\"Håkon Eriksen Drange\",\"title\":\"Principal Cloud Architect\",\"avatar\":\"/images/quotes/hakon-eriksen-drange.png\",\"externalUrl\":\"\"},{\"text\":\"Kiro is a strong ally for startups. It naturally turns overlooked docs and specs into robust assets, making growth smoother and future scaling more effective.\",\"author\":\"Kento Ikeda\",\"title\":\"Founder \u0026 Engineer\",\"avatar\":\"/images/quotes/kento-ikeda.png\",\"externalUrl\":\"\"},{\"text\":\"I use Kiro for everything - drafting new Terraform modules, tweaking container setups, even writing down random AI ideas at 2 am. But more than anything, it supports how I learn. I’m endlessly curious, and Kiro helps me stay in that learning loop—tinkering, breaking things, fixing them, and then allowing me to share what I learn back with the community.\",\"author\":\"Adit Modi\",\"title\":\"Solution Architect\",\"avatar\":\"/images/quotes/adit-modi.png\",\"externalUrl\":\"\"},{\"text\":\"I’ve been blown away by Kiro’s capabilities. The agentic experience is really transformative. From the multimodal inputs that understand context to the complete lifecycle control within the IDE, it feels like I’m working with a senior developer.\",\"author\":\"Vivek Velso\",\"title\":\"Cloud Engineering Lead\",\"avatar\":\"/images/quotes/vivek-velso.png\",\"externalUrl\":\"http://dev.to/vivek-aws\"},{\"text\":\"Most tools are great at generating code, but Kiro gives structure to the chaos before you write a single line.\",\"author\":\"Farah Abdirahman\",\"title\":\"Cloud \u0026 AI Engineer\",\"avatar\":\"/images/quotes/farah-abdirahman.png\",\"externalUrl\":\"https://medium.com/abdifarah076\"},{\"text\":\"In roughly two days, I built a secure file sharing application from scratch. By simply sharing my requirements with Kiro, I was able to create a fully secure application that incorporates encryption and various security coding practices—no additional prompts needed.\",\"author\":\"Ihor Sasovets\",\"title\":\"Lead Security Engineer\",\"avatar\":\"/images/quotes/ihor-sasovets.png\",\"externalUrl\":\"https://github.com/igorsasovets\"},{\"text\":\"I often forget to add unit tests, or update documentation when pushing changes, but with Kiro I can create a hook and it will automatically run those tasks in the background for me, never having to think twice.\",\"author\":\"Darya Petrashka\",\"title\":\"Senior Data Scientist\",\"avatar\":\"/images/quotes/darya-petrashka.png\",\"externalUrl\":\"https://github.com/dashapetr\"},{\"text\":\"As an open source developer, I typically don’t build games, but last night I was able to create one using Kiro. I didn’t have to think too hard about implementation since Kiro handled the logic and enabled me to write code quickly and accurately.\",\"author\":\"Michael Walmsley\",\"title\":\"Serverless Application Architect\",\"avatar\":\"/images/quotes/michael-walmsley.png\",\"externalUrl\":\"https://github.com/walmsles\"},{\"text\":\"Kiro lowers the barrier to entry, whether that be, you don’t even know how to code, you’re new to coding, or you’re like me and you’re just learning a new tech stack. If something doesn’t make sense to you, or you feel like you are asking a silly question, ask Kiro anyway!\",\"author\":\"Danielle Heberling\",\"title\":\"Founding Infrastructure Engineer\",\"avatar\":\"/images/quotes/danielle-heberling.png\",\"externalUrl\":\"https://github.com/deeheber\"},{\"text\":\"Kiro comes equipped with all the best practices that I would put in my specs, and builds me the application I want, faster.\",\"author\":\"Blake Romano\",\"title\":\"Senior Software Engineer\",\"avatar\":\"/images/quotes/blake-romano.png\",\"externalUrl\":\"https://github.com/blakeromano\"},{\"text\":\"Thanks to Kiro’s spec-driven development, I was able to go from concept to working prototype in a single weekend.\",\"author\":\"Dakota Lewallen\",\"title\":\"Software Engineer\",\"avatar\":\"/images/quotes/dakota-lewallen.png\",\"externalUrl\":\"https://github.com/IamFlowZ\"},{\"text\":\"In just four lines into a spec, Kiro was able to write user stories like a product manager and capture so many details that I didn’t even need to mention, saving me several days of work.\",\"author\":\"Sathiesh Veera\",\"title\":\"Principal Software Engineer\",\"externalUrl\":\"https://github.com/Sathyvs\",\"avatar\":\"/images/quotes/sathiesh-veera.png\"},{\"text\":\"Kiro isn’t just speeding up my work – it’s making me write better code, think more systematically about architecture, and deliver higher quality results while automated workflows happen seamlessly in the background.\",\"author\":\"Anuj Tyagi\",\"title\":\"Developer\",\"externalUrl\":\"https://www.linkedin.com/in/anujtyagi1/\",\"avatar\":\"/images/quotes/anuj-tyagi.png\"},{\"text\":\"Kiro is just more than code. Aside from just listening to prompts and doing things, it can actually work on tasks based on predefined triggers, making the development experience more efficient.\",\"author\":\"Sreekesh Iyer\",\"title\":\"Software Engineer\",\"avatar\":\"/images/quotes/sreekesh-iyer.png\",\"externalUrl\":\"https://blog.sreekeshiyer.com/\"}]}]}]\n"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"38:[\"$\",\"div\",\"page-component-5\",{\"className\":\"w-full IDE is for active, local development with real-time agent collaboration. Kiro CLI is for terminal-based workflows, custom agents, and deployment pipelines. Kiro Crew is the persistent, open source development workspace that coordinates across your unique tools and workflows. [Kiro Web](/web/) is for delegating and steering from the browser, with sessions that run in isolated cloud [sandboxes](/docs/web/sandbox/), keep going when you close your laptop, and coordinate changes across [GitHub](/docs/web/github/) and [GitLab](/docs/web/gitlab/) repos in a single session. All three modes ([autonomous](/docs/web/autonomous-mode/), [spec](/docs/specs/), and default) are available together in Kiro on the web. [Cloud automations](/docs/web/automations/) are only available in Kiro on the web today. Use whichever Kiro interface fits the work; they work best together. [Steering files](/docs/web/steering/) carry across all surfaces, and personal configuration syncs through [cloud configuration](/docs/web/cloud-configuration/), so your setup follows you regardless of where you work.\n"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"44:[\"$\",\"$1\",\"6\",{\"children\":[\"$L49\",[\"$\",\"$L4a\",null,{\"questions\":[{\"question\":\"What is Kiro?\",\"answer\":\"Kiro is an agentic AI with an [IDE](/docs/), [CLI](/docs/cli/), [web interface](/docs/web/), mobile app, and Kiro Crew that helps developers and teams do their best work. From quick fixes to complex tasks, Kiro works alongside you to turn prompts into executable specs, validate code correctness to find bugs unit tests miss, and build across large codebases with parallel agents that learn from every session—so what you build is exactly what you want and ready to share with your team. Kiro agents help you solve challenging problems, automate tasks, and keep working in the cloud when you step away.\\n\",\"tags\":[\"home\",\"ide\"]},{\"question\":\"What is spec-driven development? How is it different from vibe coding?\",\"answer\":\"Developing with specs keeps the fun of vibe coding, but fixes some of its limitations: vibe coding can require too much guidance on complex tasks or when building on top of large codebases, and it can misinterpret context. When implementing a task with vibe coding, it’s difficult to keep track of all the decisions that were made along the way, and document them for your team. By using specs, Kiro works alongside you to define requirements, system design, and tasks to be implemented before writing any code. This approach explicitly documents the reasoning and implementation decisions, so Kiro can implement more complex tasks in fewer shots.\\n\",\"tags\":[\"home\",\"ide\"]},{\"question\":\"How can I get started with Kiro?\",\"answer\":\"Kiro works as an agent in the [IDE](/docs/), with a [CLI](/docs/cli/), in a [web browser](/docs/web/), from mobile app, and in Kiro Crew. [Download](/downloads/) the installer for your operating system, or [sign in](https://app.kiro.dev/) to use it with a paid account on the web or mobile. Log in with GitHub, Google, AWS Builder ID, or AWS IAM Identity Center. You do not need an AWS account to use Kiro. Kiro Crew is an open-source development workspace that runs on your machine and keeps working across sessions, it’s free, install from [GitHub](https://github.com/kirodotdev/KiroCrew). For more, see [documentation](/docs).\\n\",\"tags\":[\"home\",\"ide\"]},{\"question\":\"What programming languages does Kiro support?\",\"answer\":\"Kiro supports a variety of programming languages that developers use in their day to day work. This list includes, but is not limited to Python, Java, JavaScript, TypeScript, C#, Go, Rust, PHP, Ruby, Kotlin, C, C++, shell scripting, SQL, Scala, JSON, YAML, and HCL.\\n\",\"tags\":[\"home\",\"ide\"]},{\"question\":\"Can I import settings from my existing IDE?\",\"answer\":\"Kiro is based on Code OSS, so you can [import your VS Code settings, themes, and Open VSX compatible plugins](/docs/guides/migrating-from-vscode/) in the Kiro onboarding flow.\\n\",\"tags\":[\"home\",\"ide\"]},{\"question\":\"How is Kiro Web different from Kiro IDE, CLI, and Kiro Crew?\",\"answer\":\"$4b\",\"tags\":[\"home\",\"web\"]},{\"question\":\"What is an AI agent?\",\"answer\":\"An agent is an AI system that can plan and execute multi-step tasks with some degree of autonomy. Unlike simple chatbots that respond to individual prompts, agents can break down complex tasks, make decisions about how to proceed, and take actions to accomplish goals.\\n\",\"tags\":[\"home\",\"ide\"],\"omitFromMain\":true}],\"title\":\"Common questions\",\"showCTA\":true,\"ctaHref\":\"/faq\",\"ctaText\":\"$undefined\",\"secondaryCtaHref\":\"$undefined\",\"secondaryCtaText\":\"$undefined\",\"anchor\":\"faqs\",\"customSpacing\":\"$undefined\"}]]}]\n"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"48:[\"$\",\"defs\",null,{\"children\":[\"$\",\"clipPath\",null,{\"id\":\"clip0_2878_4010\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"path\",null,{\"d\":\"M0 32C0 14.3269 14.3269 0 32 0H149C166.673 0 181 14.3269 181 32V149C181 166.673 166.673 181 149 181H32C14.3269 181 0 166.673 0 149V32Z\",\"fill\":\"white\"}]}]}]\n4c:T172e,"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"{\"@context\":\"https:\\u002F\\u002Fschema.org\",\"@type\":\"FAQPage\",\"mainEntity\":[{\"@type\":\"Question\",\"name\":\"What is Kiro?\",\"acceptedAnswer\":{\"@type\":\"Answer\",\"text\":\"\\u003Cp\\u003EKiro is an agentic AI with an \\u003Ca href=\\\"\\u002Fdocs\\u002F\\\"\\u003EIDE\\u003C\\u002Fa\\u003E, \\u003Ca href=\\\"\\u002Fdocs\\u002Fcli\\u002F\\\"\\u003ECLI\\u003C\\u002Fa\\u003E, \\u003Ca href=\\\"\\u002Fdocs\\u002Fweb\\u002F\\\"\\u003Eweb interface\\u003C\\u002Fa\\u003E, mobile app, and Kiro Crew that helps developers and teams do their best work.
